\displaystyle:=\|x-x^{\prime}\|+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{L}_{1}\omega(\|\mathfrak{R}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\|)ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-s)\varepsilon;s)\|\mathfrak{R}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\|ds\quad\mbox{for all}\quad t\in[0,1].

Clearly, for all
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{L}_{1}\omega(\|\mathfrak{R}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}(t,x^{\prime})\|)+\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-t)\varepsilon)\|\mathfrak{R}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}(t,x^{\prime})\|

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{L}_{1}\omega(V(t))+\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-t)\varepsilon;t)V(t)

\displaystyle\leq(\mathfrak{L}_{1}+\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-t)\varepsilon;t))(\omega(V(t))+V(t)).

This implies that

\frac{V^{\prime}(t)}{V(t)+\omega(V(t))}\leq\mathfrak{L}_{1}+\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-t)\varepsilon;t)\quad\mbox{for all}\quad t\in[0,1].

(E.84)

Equivalently,

\Psi(V(t))-\Psi(V(0))\leq\mathfrak{L}_{1}t+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-s)\varepsilon;s)ds,\quad\mbox{for all}\quad t\in[0,1].

(E.85)

From (
46
), setting
\mathfrak{C}=3\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)5^{d+1}/\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}
, we get

\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-s)\varepsilon;s)=\frac{3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{(1-s)\varepsilon}\times\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if }s\leq\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
1/s,&\mbox{if }\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega))\leq s\leq 1/2,\\
1/(1-s),&\mbox{if }s\geq 1/2.\end{cases}

This yields (for
t\geq 1/2
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{L}_{2}((1-s)\varepsilon;s)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{3t\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\varepsilon}\left[\int_{0}^{1/2}(1-s)^{-1}ds+\int_{\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega))}^{1/2}s^{-1}ds+\int_{1/2}^{t}(1-s)^{-2}ds\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{3t\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\varepsilon}\left[\ln\left(\frac{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{2t-1}{1-t}\right].

Noting
V(0)=\|x-x^{\prime}\|
, we conclude that, for all
t\in[1/2,1]
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|\mathfrak{R}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\|\\
&\quad\leq V(t)\leq~\Psi^{-1}\left(\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)+\mathfrak{L}_{1}+\frac{3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\ln(1+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)/\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}t}{\varepsilon(1-t)}\right)\\
&\quad\leq\Psi^{-1}\left(\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)+\mathfrak{C}_{1}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\ln(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)/\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\varepsilon}\frac{t}{1-t}\right),\end{split}

(E.86)

for some constant
\mathfrak{C}_{1}
.
Suppose

\|x-x^{\prime}\|\leq\Psi^{-1}(-2\mathfrak{C}/\varepsilon),

(E.87)

so that
\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)\leq-2\mathfrak{C}/{\varepsilon}.

Set
t^{*}\in[1/2,1)
such that

(2\mathfrak{C}/{\varepsilon})\frac{t^{*}}{1-t^{*}}=-\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)\quad\equiv\quad t^{*}=\frac{-\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)}{2\mathfrak{C}/{\varepsilon}-\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

(E.88)

Inequality (
E.86
) with
t=t^{*}
, now, implies

\sup_{s\in[0,t^{*}]}\|\mathfrak{R}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\|~\leq~\Psi^{-1}(\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)/2+\mathfrak{C}_{1}+\mathfrak{C}\ln(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)/\varepsilon)/\varepsilon).

To bound the difference for
t>t^{*}
, we use the fact that
\|v(s,z)\|\leq\mbox{diam}(\Omega)
to conclude

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\,\|\mathfrak{R}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}(t,x^{\prime})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{R}(t^{*},x)-\mathfrak{R}(t^{*},x^{\prime})\|+2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)|1-t^{*}|

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{-1}(\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)/2)+2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\frac{2\mathfrak{C}}{2\mathfrak{C}-\varepsilon\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)}.

Finally, if (
E.87
) is not satisfied, then using the fact that
\mathfrak{R}(t,x),\mathfrak{R}(t,x^{\prime})\in\Omega
, we obtain that

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\mathfrak{R}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}(t,x^{\prime})\|\leq\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\leq\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\frac{\|x-x^{\prime}\|}{\Psi^{-1}(-2\mathfrak{C}/{\varepsilon})}.

Combining all these inequalities, we conclude the first part of the result.

To prove the result for
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\gamma}^{c}\cap\Omega^{-\varepsilon}
, we note from the proof of Lemma
10
that

\mathfrak{R}(s,x),\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\in\Omega^{-(1-s)\varepsilon}\quad\mbox{for}\quad 0\leq s\leq 1-\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)},

and

\mathfrak{R}(s,x),\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\in\Omega^{-(\gamma-\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)(1-s))}\quad\mbox{for}\quad 1-\frac{\gamma}{\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}\leq s\leq 1,

Note that
\gamma\leq\mbox{diam}(\Omega)/2
and hence
1-\gamma/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega))\geq 1/2
.
This implies (using (
44
)) that

\|v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))-v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime}))\|\leq\mathfrak{L}_{1}\omega(\|\mathfrak{R}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\|)+\overline{\mathfrak{L}}_{2}(s)\|\mathfrak{R}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\|,

(E.89)

where

\begin{split}\overline{\mathfrak{L}}_{2}(s)&:=\frac{3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}\\
&+\mathfrak{C}\times\begin{cases}((1-s)\varepsilon)^{-1},&\mbox{if }s\leq\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
((1-s)s\varepsilon)^{-1},&\mbox{if }\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega))\leq s\leq 1/2,\\
((1-s)^{2}\varepsilon)^{-1},&\mbox{if }1/2\leq s\leq 1-\gamma/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
((\gamma-\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)(1-s))(1-s))^{-1},&\mbox{if }1-\gamma/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega))\leq s\leq 1-\gamma/(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
(\gamma-\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)(1-s))^{-1},&\mbox{if }s\geq 1-\gamma/(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)).\end{cases}\end{split}

From this expression, we also derive

\int_{0}^{1}\overline{\mathfrak{L}}_{2}(s)ds=\frac{3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\varepsilon}\left[\ln\left(\frac{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)+\varepsilon-2\gamma}{\gamma}\right]+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\gamma}\ln\left(\frac{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\ln 2}{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}.

Hence, (following the proof of) (
E.85
) implies

\Psi(V(1))-\Psi(V(0))\leq\mathfrak{L}_{1}+\frac{3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\ln 2}{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}+\frac{2\mathfrak{C}}{\min\{\varepsilon,\gamma\}}\ln\left(\frac{2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{2\mathfrak{C}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon\gamma}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\,\|\mathfrak{R}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x^{\prime})\|

\displaystyle\leq~\Psi^{-1}\left(\Psi(\|x-x^{\prime}\|)+\mathfrak{L}_{1}+\frac{3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\omega^{-1}(1)}+\frac{\mathfrak{C}\ln 2}{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}+\frac{2\mathfrak{C}}{\min\{\varepsilon,\gamma\}}\ln\left(\frac{2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{2\mathfrak{C}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon\gamma}\right).

S.6.5
Proof of Theorem
10

Fix any
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathcal{V}
such that
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta
.
Applying (
58
) with
\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left\{\nu_{1}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x))-\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x))\right\}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\{\nu_{1}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x))-\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x))\}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\{\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x))-\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x))\}ds.

This implies that for
t\in[0,1]

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(t,x)\|\leq\Delta+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x))-\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x))\|ds.

To control the second term on the right hand side, observe that Lemma
9
implies that for all
\nu\in\mathcal{V}
,
\mathfrak{R}_{\nu}(s,x)\in\Omega^{-(1-s)\varepsilon}
for all
s\in[0,1]
. Therefore, property
(P2)
implies

\begin{split}&\|\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x))-\nu_{2}(s,\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x))\|\\
&\leq\mathfrak{C}\omega(\|\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x)\|)\\
&\quad+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{(1-s)\varepsilon}\|\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x)\|\times\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if }s\leq\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
1/s,&\mbox{if }\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega))\leq s\leq 1/2,\\
1/(1-s),&\mbox{if }s\geq 1/2.\end{cases}\end{split}

(E.90)

Set

\displaystyle V(t)

\displaystyle=\Delta+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{C}\omega(\|\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x)\|)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{L}(s;\varepsilon)\|\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(s,x)\|ds,

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}(\varepsilon;s):=\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{(1-s)\varepsilon}\times\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if }s\leq\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
1/s,&\mbox{if }\varepsilon/(\varepsilon+\mbox{diam}(\Omega))\leq s\leq 1/2,\\
1/(1-s),&\mbox{if }s\geq 1/2.\end{cases}

This implies

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(t)\leq\mathfrak{L}(\varepsilon,t)(\omega(V(t))+V(t)).

Now following the proof of Theorem
9
(after (
E.84
)), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathcal{V},\,x\in\Omega^{-\varepsilon}\\
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta\end{subarray}}\,\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\,\|\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{-1}(\Psi(\Delta)/2+C\ln(\mbox{diam}(\Omega)/\varepsilon)/\varepsilon)+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\max\left\{\frac{4C}{2C-\varepsilon\Psi(\Delta)},\,\frac{\Delta}{\Psi^{-1}(-C/\varepsilon)}\right\},

for some constant
C
depending only on
\mathfrak{C}
.

To prove the result under (
60
), note that the above inequality yields

\mbox{dist}(\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(1,x),\,\partial\Omega)\geq\mbox{dist}(\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{2}}(1,x),\,\partial\Omega)-\mbox{RHS of}~\eqref{eq:equicontinuity-in-velocity-weak}.

Hence, when
\Delta
is small enough so that the right hand side of (
59
) is smaller than
\gamma
, we get

\mbox{dist}(\mathfrak{R}_{\nu_{1}}(1,x),\,\partial\Omega)\geq\gamma.

This implies that inequality (
E.90
) can be improved in the second term exactly as in the proof of Theorem
9
(see inequality (
E.89
)). Hence, following that proof, the second result follows.

S.6.6
Proof of Theorem
11

For a non-negative function
h:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
, set

\displaystyle\widehat{T}_{h}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)}h(\delta)\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta,

\displaystyle{T}_{h}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)}h(\delta){p}_{0}(z-t\delta){p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta

Using (
63
), we get

e^{-2r_{n}}T_{h}(z)\leq\widehat{T}_{h}(z)\leq e^{2r_{n}}T_{h}(z)\quad\mbox{for all}\quad z\in\Omega.

(E.91)

For any vector
a\in S^{d-1}
(the unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
), we have

\displaystyle a^{\top}\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z)}a^{\top}\delta\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}{\int_{S_{t}(z)}\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z)}(a^{\top}\delta)_{+}\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}{\int_{S_{t}(z)}\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}-\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z)}(a^{\top}\delta)_{-}\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}{\int_{S_{t}(z)}\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}

\displaystyle\leq e^{4r_{n}}\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z)}(a^{\top}\delta)_{+}{p}_{0}(z-t\delta){p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}{\int_{S_{t}(z)}{p}_{0}(z-t\delta){p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}-e^{-4r_{n}}\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z)}(a^{\top}\delta)_{-}{p}_{0}(z-t\delta){p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}{\int_{S_{t}(z)}{p}_{0}(z-t\delta){p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}

\displaystyle\leq a^{\top}v(t,z)+\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}\|\delta\|\max\{|e^{4r_{n}}-1|,\,|1-e^{-4r_{n}}|\}.

The first inequality follows from (
E.91
) with
h(\delta)=(a^{\top}\delta)_{+}
,
h(\delta)=(a^{\top}\delta)_{-}
, and
h(\delta)\equiv 1
. The second inequality follows from the fact that, for all
z\in\Omega^{\circ}
,

\frac{T_{h}(z)}{p_{t}(z)}\leq\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}|h(\delta)|.

Swapping the roles of
\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}
and
v
, we get

\sup_{t\in[0,1],z\in\Omega^{\circ}}\|\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)-v(t,z)\|\leq\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}\|\delta\|\max\{|e^{4r_{n}}-1|,\,|1-e^{-4r_{n}}|\}\leq 2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)|e^{4r_{n}}-1|.

This completes the proof by noting that
\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}\|\delta\|\leq 2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)
.

S.6.7
Proof of Lemma
11

Fix any
z,z^{\prime}\in\Omega^{-\varepsilon}
with
\eta=\|z-z^{\prime}\|.
Set

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)}\delta\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta

\displaystyle{f}_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)}\delta{p}_{0}(z-t\delta){p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)}\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta

\displaystyle{p}_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)}{p}_{0}(z-t\delta){p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta

Note that

\displaystyle m_{t}(z,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)-v(t,z)\right\}-\left\{\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})-v(t,z^{\prime})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{f}_{t}(z)-\widehat{f}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}-\frac{f_{t}(z)-f_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z)}+\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})\left(\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}-1\right)-v(t,z^{\prime})\left(\frac{p_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z)}-1\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{p_{t}(z)}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}-1\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{f}_{t}(z)-\widehat{f}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z)}\right)+\frac{\{\widehat{f}_{t}(z)-\widehat{f}_{t}(z^{\prime})\}-\{f_{t}(z)-f_{t}(z^{\prime})\}}{p_{t}(z)}

\displaystyle\quad+(\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})-v(t,z^{\prime}))\left(\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}-1\right)

\displaystyle\quad+v(t,z^{\prime})\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}\frac{\{p_{t}(z)-p_{t}(z^{\prime})\}-\{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)-\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})\}}{p_{t}(z)}+v(t,z^{\prime})\frac{p_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z)}\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z^{\prime})}-1\right).

(For a more detailed proof, see
https://drive.google.com/file/d/1gXQsVFYonEB9eHqq52vlUHnpgffNojdi/view?usp=sharing
.)
Note that the first term can be further decomposed as

\displaystyle\left(\frac{p_{t}(z)}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}-1\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{f}_{t}(z)-\widehat{f}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}{p_{t}(z)}\right)\left(\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)-\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}{p_{t}(z)}\right)\left(\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)-\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}{p_{t}(z)}\right)\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}\right).

Every term in the decomposition has one factor related to the closeness of
z,z^{\prime}
, and the other related to the closeness of
(\widehat{p}_{0},\widehat{p}_{1})
and
(p_{0},p_{1})
.

We first note a few simple inequalities that control most of these terms. Under the assumption that
\omega(\kappa)\leq L\kappa
for all
\kappa>0
and
\eta\leq\min\{1/L,\,\varepsilon^{2}/\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\}
, we get

\begin{split}\max\{\|v(t,z)\|,\,\|v(t,z^{\prime})\|,\,\|\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)\|,\,\|\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})\|\}~&\leq~\mbox{diam}(\Omega),\\
\max\left\{\left|\frac{p_{t}(z)}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}-1\right|,\,\left|\frac{p_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}-1\right|,\,\left|\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}{p_{t}(z)}-1\right|,\,\left|\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z^{\prime})}-1\right|\right\}~&\leq~e^{2r_{n}}-1,\\
\|\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)-v(t,z)\|~&\leq~2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)(e^{4r_{n}}-1),\\
\max\left\{\left|\frac{p_{t}(z)}{p_{t}(z^{\prime})}-1\right|,\,\left|\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}-1\right|,\,\left|\frac{p_{t}(z^{\prime})}{p_{t}(z)}-1\right|,\,\left|\frac{\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})}{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)}-1\right|\right\}~&\leq~\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon),\\
\|\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z)-\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(t,z^{\prime})\|~&\leq~3\eta\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon),\end{split}

(E.92)

where

\displaystyle\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=3L+\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\varepsilon}\times\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if }\min\{t,1-t\}\leq\varepsilon/\mbox{diam}(\Omega),\\
1/\min\{t,1-t\},&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3L\varepsilon^{2}+\mathfrak{C}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\leq\frac{3L\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)+\mathfrak{C}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon^{2}}=:\frac{C_{1}}{\varepsilon^{2}},

with the constant
C_{1}
depending only on
d,L,\mbox{diam}(\Omega),
and
(\overline{\mathfrak{p}},\,\underline{\mathfrak{p}})
.
These inequalities follow from (
43
), (
E.91
) (with
h\equiv 1
), Theorem
11
, (
E.69
) (with
h\equiv 1
), and (
44
) (along with (
46
)). These inequalities imply that

\displaystyle\|m_{t}(z,z^{\prime})\|

\displaystyle\leq 3(e^{2r_{n}}-1)\eta\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)(e^{2r_{n}}-1)\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\|\{\widehat{f}_{t}(z)-\widehat{f}_{t}(z^{\prime})\}-\{f_{t}(z)-f_{t}(z^{\prime})\}\|}{p_{t}(z)}

\displaystyle\quad+2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\eta(e^{4r_{n}}-1)\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\quad+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)(1+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon))\frac{|\{p_{t}(z)-p_{t}(z^{\prime})\}-\{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)-\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})\}|}{p_{t}(z)}

\displaystyle\quad+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)(1+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon))\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)(e^{2r_{n}}-1)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\eta(e^{4r_{n}}-1)\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)(1+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\|\{\widehat{f}_{t}(z)-\widehat{f}_{t}(z^{\prime})\}-\{f_{t}(z)-f_{t}(z^{\prime})\}\|}{p_{t}(z)}

\displaystyle\quad+\mbox{diam}(\Omega)(1+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon))\frac{|\{p_{t}(z)-p_{t}(z^{\prime})\}-\{\widehat{p}_{t}(z)-\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})\}|}{p_{t}(z)}.

for some constant
C_{2}
depending only on
\mbox{diam}(\Omega).
To bound the last two terms, consider

\displaystyle\Delta(h)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z)}h(\delta)\{p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)\}d\delta}{p_{t}(z)}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z^{\prime})}h(\delta)\{p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)p_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)\}d\delta}{p_{t}(z)}.

Bounding
\Delta(h)
for
h(\delta)=\delta
and
h(\delta)\equiv 1
provides bounds for the last two terms of
\|m_{t}(z,z^{\prime})\|
.
We shall split
\Delta(h)
into integrals on
S_{t}(z)\cap S_{t}(z^{\prime})
,
S_{t}(z)\setminus S_{t}(z^{\prime})
, and
S_{t}(z^{\prime})\setminus S_{t}(z)
, as in the proof of Lemma
8
.
The integrand on
S_{t}(z)\cap S_{t}(z^{\prime})
without the factor of
h(\delta)
can be decomposed as

\begin{split}&(p_{0}(z-t\delta)-p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)+\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta))p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)\\
&\quad+(\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta))(p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)-\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta))\\
&\quad+p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)(p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)-p_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)-\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)+\widehat{p}_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta))\\
&\quad+(p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta))(\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)-\widehat{p}_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)).\end{split}

(E.93)

The second and the fourth terms of (
E.93
) can be upper-bounded by

L\|z-z^{\prime}\|(e^{r_{n}}-1)\left[\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)+\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)\right].

The first and the third terms of (
E.93
) can be upper-bounded using the log-derivatives of the densities.
Note that these are well-defined only for
x\in\Omega^{\circ}
. We write

\displaystyle p_{0}(z-t\delta)-p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)+\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)

\displaystyle=p_{0}(z-t\delta)\left(1-\frac{p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{p_{0}(z-t\delta)}\right)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)}\right)

\displaystyle=p_{0}(z-t\delta)\left(\frac{\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)}-\frac{p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{p_{0}(z-t\delta)}\right)+p_{0}(z-t\delta)\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)}{p_{0}(z-t\delta)}\right)\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)}\right).

Hence, the first term can be upper-bounded by

\displaystyle p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)\left[(1+L\eta)(\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1)+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right]

\displaystyle\leq p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)\left[2(\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1)+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right].

This follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)}-\frac{p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{p_{0}(z-t\delta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)}{p_{0}(z-t\delta)}\left(\exp\left(\log\widehat{p}_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)-\log\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)-\log p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)+\log p_{0}(z-t\delta)\right)-1\right).

Similarly, the third term of (
E.93
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)p_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)\left[(1+L\eta)(\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1)+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right]

\displaystyle\leq p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)p_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)\left[2(\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1)+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right].

Combining these bounds, we conclude that the contribution to
\Delta(h)
from the integration on
S_{t}(z)\cap S_{t}(z^{\prime})
is bounded by

\begin{split}&\frac{\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)\cap S_{t}(z^{\prime})}\|\delta\|}{p_{t}(z)}L\eta(e^{r_{n}}-1)\left(\widehat{p}_{t}(z)+\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})\right)\\
&\quad+\frac{\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)\cap S_{t}(z^{\prime})}\|\delta\|}{p_{t}(z)}\left[2(\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1)+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right]\left(p_{t}(z)+p_{t}(z^{\prime})\right).\end{split}

(E.94)

Because
\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)\cap S_{t}(z^{\prime})}\|\delta\|\leq 2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)
and
\widehat{p}_{t}(z)/p_{t}(z),\widehat{p}_{t}(z^{\prime})/p_{t}(z),p_{t}(z^{\prime})/p_{t}(z)
are all bounded using (
E.92
), we get the simplified bound on the intersection integral as

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{C}\left(2+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)\right)\left[2(\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1)+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right].\end{split}

(E.95)

To control the integral over
S_{t}(z)\setminus S_{t}(z^{\prime})
, note that

\displaystyle|p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)-\widehat{p}_{0}(z-t\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)|

\displaystyle\leq(e^{2r_{n}}-1)\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2},

and hence, the second term can be bounded by

\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}\,|h(\delta)|\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}\frac{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z)\setminus S_{t}(z^{\prime}))}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}(e^{2r_{n}}-1).

(E.96)

Similarly, the third term can be bounded by

\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}\,|h(\delta)|\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}\frac{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z^{\prime})\setminus S_{t}(z))}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}(e^{2r_{n}}-1).

(E.97)

Together, the second and third terms can be bounded by

\begin{split}&\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}\,|h(\delta)|\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}\frac{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z)\Delta S_{t}(z^{\prime}))}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}(e^{2r_{n}}-1)\\
&\leq~\mathfrak{C}(e^{2r_{n}}-1)\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon}\times\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if }\min\{t,1-t\}\leq\varepsilon/\mbox{diam}(\Omega),\\
1/\min\{t,1-t\},&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}\\
&\leq~\mathfrak{C}(e^{2r_{n}}-1)\frac{\eta\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon^{2}}.\end{split}

(E.98)

Combining bounds (
E.95
), (
E.98
) and the bound on
\|m_{t}(z,z^{\prime})\|
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|m_{t}(z,z^{\prime})\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\eta(e^{4r_{n}}-1)\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon)(1+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\quad+C_{3}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)(1+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon))^{2}\left[2(\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1)+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{4}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)(1+\eta\mbox{Lip}_{t}(\eta,\varepsilon))(e^{2r_{n}}-1)\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon}\times\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if }\min\{t,1-t\}\leq\varepsilon/\mbox{diam}(\Omega),\\
1/\min\{t,1-t\},&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\eta(e^{4r_{n}}-1)(\varepsilon^{-2}+\eta\varepsilon^{-4})

\displaystyle\quad+C_{6}(1+\eta\varepsilon^{-2})^{2}\left[\exp(s_{n}\eta)-1+(e^{r_{n}}-1)L\eta\right],

for some constants
C_{5}
and
C_{6}
depending only on
d,L,\mbox{diam}(\Omega),
and
(\overline{\mathfrak{p}},\,\underline{\mathfrak{p}})
.

S.6.8
Proof of Proposition
4

For
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, define

T_{t}(z)=\frac{\int_{S_{t}(z)}g(\delta)p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}{\int_{S_{t}(z)}p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}.

Note that

\displaystyle T_{t}(z)-T_{t+h}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)}g(\delta)p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta-\int_{S_{t+h}(z)}g(\delta)p_{0}(z-(t+h)\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t-h)\delta)d\delta

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z)\cap S_{t+h}(z)}g(\delta)p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)\left[1-\frac{p_{0}(z-(t+h)\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t-h)\delta)}{p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)}\right]d\delta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{S_{t}(z)\setminus S_{t+h}(z)}g(\delta)p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{S_{t+h}(z)\setminus S_{t}(z)}g(\delta)p_{0}(z-(t+h)\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t-h)\delta)d\delta.

From the modulus of continuity of the densities, we get

\displaystyle\left|1-\frac{p_{0}(z-(t+h)\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t-h)\delta)}{p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)}\right|~

\displaystyle\leq~(1+\omega^{2}(h\|\delta\|))-1\leq 2Lh\|\delta\|+L^{2}h^{2}\|\delta\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~4Lh\mbox{diam}(\Omega)+4L^{2}h^{2}\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\leq~8L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)h,\quad\mbox{if }\quad h\leq 1/(L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)).

This implies that
t\mapsto 1-{p_{0}(z-(t+h)\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t-h)\delta)}/{p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)}
is Lipschitz continuous with a Lipschitz constant of
8L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)
for all
t\in(0,1)
. Therefore, the bound holds for all
h>0
such that
t+h\in[0,1]
.
Hence, the first term in the decomposition of
T_{t}(z)-T_{t+h}(z)
is bounded by

p_{t}(z)\left(\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}|g(\delta)|\right)8L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)h.

The second and third terms in the decompositions are together bounded by

\left(\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}|g(\delta)|\right)\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z)\Delta S_{t+h}(z)).

Because
p_{t}(z)\geq\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))
, we get

\frac{|T_{t}(z)-T_{t+h}(z)|}{p_{t}(z)}\leq\left(\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}|g(\delta)|\right)\left(8L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)h+\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}\frac{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z)\Delta S_{t+h}(z))}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}\right).

To control the volume of the symmetric difference, we consider two cases.

Case 1:
\min\{t,1-t\}\leq\varepsilon/(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega))
.

In this case, using the assumption that
h\leq\varepsilon/(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega))
, we get
\min\{t+h,1-t-h\}\leq\varepsilon/\mbox{diam}(\Omega)
. Hence, Proposition
10
(1) implies

\displaystyle S_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(x-\Omega)/t,&\mbox{if }t\geq\varepsilon/(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
(\Omega-x)/(1-t),&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}\;\mbox{and}

\displaystyle S_{t+h}(z)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(x-\Omega)/(t+h),&\mbox{if }t\geq\varepsilon/(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\\
(\Omega-x)/(1-t-h),&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z)\Delta S_{t+h}(z))}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\left|1-\frac{t}{t+h}\right|,\,\left|1-\frac{1-t}{1-t-h}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{h}{\min\{t+h,1-t-h\}}\leq\frac{\mbox{diam}(\Omega)h}{\varepsilon}.

Case 2:
\min\{t,1-t\}>\varepsilon/(2\mbox{diam}(\Omega))
.

In this case, Proposition
10
(4) implies that

\frac{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t+h}(z)\Delta S_{t}(z))}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}\leq\frac{\mbox{Vol}((S_{t}(z))^{2\mathrm{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h/(\varepsilon\bar{t})}\setminus(S_{t}(z))^{-2\mathrm{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h/(\varepsilon\bar{t})})}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}.

If
2\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h/(\varepsilon\bar{t})\leq\varepsilon/(2\max\{t,1-t\})
(which is satisfied if
h\leq\varepsilon^{3}/(4\mbox{diam}^{3}(\Omega))
), then Lemma
14
implies

\displaystyle\frac{\mbox{Vol}((S_{t}(z))^{2\mathrm{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h/(\varepsilon\bar{t})}\setminus(S_{t}(z))^{-2\mathrm{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h/(\varepsilon\bar{t})})}{\mbox{Vol}((S_{t}(z))^{-2\mathrm{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h/(\varepsilon\bar{t})})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8d\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h\max\{t,1-t\}}{\varepsilon^{2}\bar{t}}\left(1+\frac{8\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h\max\{t,1-t\}}{\varepsilon^{2}\bar{t}}\right)^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4^{d+1}d\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h\max\{t,1-t\}}{\varepsilon^{2}\bar{t}}.

Therefore,

\frac{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t+h}(z)\Delta S_{t}(z))}{\mbox{Vol}(S_{t}(z))}\leq\frac{4^{d+1}d\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)h}{\varepsilon^{2}\bar{t}},\quad\mbox{if }h\leq\varepsilon^{3}/(4\mbox{diam}^{3}(\Omega)).

Combining both cases, we conclude

\frac{|T_{t}(z)-T_{t+h}(z)|}{p_{t}(z)}\leq\left(\sup_{\delta\in S_{t}(z)}|g(\delta)|\right)\left(8L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)+\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}\frac{4^{d+1}d\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon^{2}\bar{t}}\right)h,

whenever
h\leq\varepsilon^{3}/(4\mbox{diam}^{3}(\Omega))
. Taking
g(\delta)=e_{j}^{\top}\delta
and
g(\delta)\equiv v(t,z)
, we get

\begin{split}\|v(t,z)-v(t+h,z)\|&\leq\frac{\|f_{t}(z)-f_{t+h}(z)\|}{p_{t}(z)}+\|v(t+h,z)\|\left|\frac{p_{t+h}(z)}{p_{t}(z)}-1\right|\\
&\leq 2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\left(8L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)+\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}\frac{4^{d+1}d\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon^{2}\bar{t}}\right)h,\end{split}

(E.99)

whenever
h\leq\min\{1/(L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)),\,\varepsilon^{3}/(4\mbox{diam}^{3}(\Omega))\}
. This implies that
t\mapsto v(t,z)
is almost everywhere differentiable on
(0,1)
, with derivative bounded by

2\mbox{diam}(\Omega)\left(8L\mbox{diam}(\Omega)+\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}}\frac{4^{d+1}d\mbox{diam}^{2}(\Omega)}{\varepsilon^{2}\bar{t}}\right).

Hence, inequality (
E.99
) holds for all
h>0
such that
t+h\in[0,1]
. This proves the result.

S.6.9
Proof of Theorem
13

Recall that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=x+\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,\,\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x))ds,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}(t,x)

\displaystyle=x+\int_{0}^{t}v(s,\,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))ds.

This implies
\widehat{E}(t,x)=\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}(t,x)
satisfies

\displaystyle\widehat{E}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\{\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x))-v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\{\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x))-v(s,\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x))-\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))+v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\{\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))-v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\{v(s,\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x))-v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\}ds.

From Lemma
11
(along with Corollary
2
), the first term can be bounded (for large enough
n
) by

C^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\gamma}\sup_{0\leq s\leq 1}\|\widehat{E}(s,x)\|(r_{n}+s_{n}).

From Lemma
10
,
\mathfrak{R}(s,x)
belongs to the set of differentiability points of
z\mapsto v(s,z)
for almost all
s\in[0,1]
and hence,

v(s,\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x))-v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))=\frac{\partial}{\partial z}v(s,z)^{\top}\bigg|_{z=\mathfrak{R}(s,x)}(\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x))+o_{p}(\|\widehat{E}(s,x)\|).

Moreover, the Lipschitz continuity of
z\mapsto v(s,z)
on
\Omega^{-\kappa}
(for any
\kappa>0
) implies that

\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\left\|v(s,\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x))-v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))-\frac{\partial}{\partial z}v(s,z)^{\top}\bigg|_{z=\mathfrak{R}(s,x)}(\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(s,x)-\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\right\|\leq C\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\widehat{E}(s,x)\|,

for some constant
C
depending on the Lipschitz constant of
z\mapsto v(s,z)
.
Therefore, by the dominated convergence theorem, we get

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{n}&:=\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\widehat{E}(t,x)-\int_{0}^{t}\{\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))-v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\}ds-\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{z}v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))^{\top}\widehat{E}(s,x)ds\right\|\\
&=o_{p}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\widehat{E}(s,x)\|\right).\end{split}

(E.100)

Then, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\,\left\|\widehat{E}(t,x)-\widetilde{E}(t,x)-\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{z}v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))^{\top}(\widehat{E}(s,x)-\widetilde{E}(s,x))ds\right\|=\mathcal{E}_{n}.

This implies

\left\|\widehat{E}(t,x)-\widetilde{E}(t,x)\right\|\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{z}v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\widehat{E}(s,x)-\widetilde{E}(s,x)\|ds.

From the Lipschitz continuity of
z\mapsto v(s,z)
, we get
\|\partial_{z}v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
. Hence, setting
V(t)=\mathcal{E}_{n}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|\widehat{E}(s,x)-\widetilde{E}(s,x)\|ds
, we have

V^{\prime}(t)=C\left\|\widehat{E}(t,x)-\widetilde{E}(t,x)\right\|\leq CV(t)\quad\mbox{for all}\quad t\in[0,1].

This implies

\frac{V(t)}{V(0)}\leq C\quad\Rightarrow\quad\left\|\widehat{E}(t,x)-\widetilde{E}(t,x)\right\|\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}e^{Ct}\quad\mbox{for all}\quad t\in[0,1].

In particular, from (
E.100
), we conclude

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\widehat{E}(t,x)-\widetilde{E}(t,x)\|=o_{p}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\widehat{E}(s,x)\|\right).

This is equivalent to

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\widehat{E}(t,x)-\widetilde{E}(t,x)\|=o_{p}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\widetilde{E}(s,x)\|\right),\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\to\infty.

This proves the result.

S.6.10
Proof of Proposition
5

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{p}_{j}(z)]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{K_{z,h}(x-z)}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}p_{j}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega-z}\frac{K_{z,h}(u)}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}p_{j}(z+u)du

Because
K_{z,h}(u)=0
whenever
\|u\|>h
, we get that

|p_{j}(z+u)-q_{j}(u)|\leq L\|u\|^{\beta}\leq Lh^{\beta}.

This implies that

\left|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{p}_{j}(z)]-\int_{V_{z,h}}\frac{K_{z,h}(u)}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}q_{z,j}(u)du\right|~\leq~Lh^{\beta}\int_{V_{z,h}}\frac{|K_{z,h}(u)|}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}du\leq Lh^{\beta}\frac{\mathfrak{K}\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}=L\mathfrak{K}h^{\beta}.

Note that assumption
2
implies
q_{z,j}(0)=p_{j}(z)
and
q_{z,j}(u)=p_{j}(z)+\sum_{1\leq\|\alpha\|_{1}\leq\llfloor s\rrfloor}a_{\alpha}\prod_{j=1}^{d}u_{j}^{\alpha_{j}}
for some coefficients
a_{\alpha}
which can depend on
z.
Hence, the assumption on the kernel yields

\int_{V_{z,h}}\frac{K_{z,h}(u)}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}q_{z,j}(u)du=p_{j}(z)\frac{\int_{V_{z,h}}K_{z,h}(u)du}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}=p_{j}(z).

Therefore,

\sup_{z\in\Omega}\,\left|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{p}_{j}(z)]-p_{j}(z)\right|~\leq~L\mathfrak{K}h^{\beta}.

The bound is uniform over all
z\in\Omega
.

To bound the variance, note that

\displaystyle\mbox{Var}(\widehat{p}_{j}(z))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n\mbox{Vol}^{2}(V_{z,h})}\mbox{Var}\left(K_{z,h}(X_{j1}-z)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n\mbox{Vol}^{2}(V_{z,h})}\int_{\Omega}K_{z,h}^{2}(x-z)p_{j}(z)dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n\mbox{Vol}^{2}(V_{z,h})}\int_{V_{z,h}}K_{z,h}^{2}(u)p_{j}(z+u)du

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{K}^{2}\|p_{j}\|_{\infty}}{n\mbox{Vol}^{2}(V_{z,h})}\int_{V_{z,h}}du=\frac{\mathfrak{K}^{2}\|p_{j}\|_{\infty}}{n\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})}.

The final part of the result follows from the fact that any set of full affine rank has positive Lebesgue density at each point and that every convex set with non-empty interior has a full affine rank. See, for example,
https://math.stackexchange.com/questions/3491213/is-there-a-lower-bound-to-density-at-boundary-points-of-a-convex-set
and
Choudhary2025Uniform
.
This completes the proof.

S.6.11
Proof of Theorem
14

Observe that

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,z)-v(s,z)

\displaystyle\quad=\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,z)\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{s}(z)}{p_{s}(z)}\right)+\frac{\widehat{f}_{s}(z)-f_{s}(z)}{p_{s}(z)}

\displaystyle\quad=v(s,z)\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{s}(z)}{p_{s}(z)}\right)+\frac{\widehat{f}_{s}(z)-f_{s}(z)}{p_{s}(z)}+(\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,z)-v(s,z))\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{s}(z)}{p_{s}(z)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{S_{s}(z)}\frac{\delta-v(s,z)}{p_{s}(z)}\{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-s\delta)\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-s)\delta)-p_{0}(z-s\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-s)\delta)\}d\delta

\displaystyle\qquad+(\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,z)-v(s,z))\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{s}(z)}{p_{s}(z)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{S_{s}(z)}\frac{\delta-v(s,z)}{p_{s}(z)}p_{1}(z+(1-s)\delta)\{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-s\delta)-p_{0}(z-s\delta)\}d\delta

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{S_{s}(z)}\frac{\delta-v(s,z)}{p_{s}(z)}p_{0}(z-s\delta)\{\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-s)\delta)-p_{1}(z+(1-s)\delta)\}\delta

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{S_{s}(z)}\frac{\delta-v(s,z)}{p_{s}(z)}\{\widehat{p}_{0}(z-s\delta)-p_{0}(z-s\delta)\}\{\widehat{p}_{1}(z+(1-s)\delta)-p_{1}(z+(1-s)\delta)\}d\delta

\displaystyle\qquad+(\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,z)-v(s,z))\left(1-\frac{\widehat{p}_{s}(z)}{p_{s}(z)}\right).

With
z_{s}=\mathfrak{R}(s,z)
, define

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\frac{\delta-v(s,z_{s})}{p_{s}(z)}p_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)\{\widehat{p}_{0}(z_{s}-s\delta)-p_{0}(z_{s}-s\delta)\}d\delta

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{S_{s}(z)}\frac{\delta-v(s,z)}{p_{s}(z_{s})}p_{0}(z_{s}-s\delta)\{\widehat{p}_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)-p_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)\}\delta.

Then we get

\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\left\|\widehat{v}^{\mathrm{den}}(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))-v(s,\,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))-\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)\right\|=O_{p}\left(\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\Phi(1)(\Phi(s))^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right)r_{n}^{2}.

(We now prove that
\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\Phi(1)(\Phi(s))^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(1)
. Set
A(s)=\partial_{z}v(s,\mathfrak{R}(s,x))^{\top}
. From Eq. (1.6) of
22
, we get
(d/dt)(\Phi(t))^{-1}=-A(t)(\Phi(t))^{-1}
for almost all
t\in[0,1]
with
(\Phi(0))^{-1}=I
. Equivalently,
(\Phi(t))^{-1}=I+\int_{0}^{t}A(s)(\Phi(s))^{-1}ds
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|(\Phi(t))^{-1}\Phi(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Phi(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(\Phi(s))^{-1}\Phi(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|A(s)\|_{\mathrm{op}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\|\Phi(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|(\Phi(s))^{-1}\Phi(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}}ds.

Solving this inequality, we get
\|(\Phi(t))^{-1}\Phi(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\Phi(1)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\exp(Ct)
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Following the same strategy with the differential equation defining
\Phi(\cdot)
, we get
\|\Phi(1)\|\leq\exp(C)
. Hence,
\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\Phi(1)(\Phi(s))^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\exp(2C)
where
C
is the (uniform) Lipschitz constant of
z\mapsto v(s,z)
over
s\in[0,1]
.)

Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)]

\displaystyle:=\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\frac{\delta-v(s,z_{s})}{p_{s}(z_{s})}p_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)\{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{p}_{0}(z_{s}-s\delta)]-p_{0}(z_{s}-s\delta)\}d\delta

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\frac{\delta-v(s,z_{s})}{p_{s}(z_{s})}p_{0}(z_{s}-s\delta)\{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{p}_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)]-p_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)\}\delta.

From Proposition
5
(under assumption
2
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)]\|

\displaystyle\leq 3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)L\mathfrak{K}h^{\beta}\int_{S_{s}(z)}\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{j}(z+(j-s)\delta)\mathbf{1}\{z+(1-j-s)\delta\in\Omega\}}{p_{s}(z)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3L\mathfrak{K}\mbox{diam}(\Omega)}{\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}h^{\beta}.

Therefore, defining

\widetilde{E}^{*}(t,x):=\Phi(t)\int_{0}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds,

we get

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}(t,x)-\mathfrak{R}(t,x)-\widetilde{E}^{*}(t,x)\right\|=o_{p}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\widetilde{E}^{*}(t,x)\|\right)+O_{p}(r_{n}^{2}+h^{\beta}).

We first find the rate of convergence of
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\widetilde{E}^{*}(t,x)\|
. Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\widetilde{E}^{*}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\,\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,t^{*}]}\,\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\,\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2C\sup_{t\in[0,t^{*}]}\,\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds\right\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\,\left\|\int_{t^{*}}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds\right\|

Set

\displaystyle Z_{n}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds,\quad\mbox{for}\quad 0\leq t\leq t^{*},

\displaystyle W_{n}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{t^{*}}^{t}(\Phi(s))^{-1}(\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\gamma}(s,x)])ds,\quad\mbox{for}\quad t^{*}\leq t\leq 1.

We apply the Kolmogorov continuity theorem (i.e., a simple version of generic chaining) to control the supremum of these terms. (The following proof follows Section 1.3 of
91
.)
We shall control
\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\|W_{n}(t)\|
first.
For each
k\geq 1
, set
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\{\lfloor 2^{k}t\rfloor/2^{k}:\,t\in[0,1)\}\cap[t^{*},1]
. Clearly, the cardinality of
\mathcal{G}_{k}
is at most
2^{k}
. For each
t\in[t^{*},1]
, choose
\pi_{k}(t)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}
such that
|t-\pi_{k}(t)|\leq 2^{-k}
. It is easy to see that
|\pi_{k}(t)-\pi_{k-1}(t)|\leq 3/2^{k}
for all
k\geq 1
and
t\in[t^{*},1]
. Set

\mathcal{U}_{k}=\{(s,t)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\times\mathcal{G}_{k}:\,|s-t|\leq 3/2^{k}\}.

Since, for any given
s\in\mathcal{G}_{k}
, there are at most six elements in
\mathcal{G}_{k}
that are
3/2^{k}
distant from
s
, we conclude that the cardinality of
\mathcal{U}_{k}
is at most
3\times 2^{k+1}
. This implies that

|\{(\pi_{k}(t),\,\pi_{k-1}(t)):\,t\in[t^{*},1]\}|\leq 3\times 2^{k+1}.

(E.101)

This holds because
\mathcal{G}_{k-1}\subset\mathcal{G}_{k}
.
Observe now that, setting
\pi_{0}(t)=t^{*}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\|W_{n}(t)\|\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\|W_{n}(\pi_{k}(t))-W_{n}(\pi_{k-1}(t))\|\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\|W_{n}(t_{2})-W_{n}(t_{1})\|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq d^{1/2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\,(3\times 2^{k+1})^{1/2}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left(\sum_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[|e_{j}^{\top}(W_{n}(t_{2})-W_{n}(t_{1})|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}.\end{split}

(E.102)

(Here we used the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\max_{1\leq j\leq M}|W_{j}|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\sum_{j}W_{j}^{2}].
)
For
t^{*}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq 1
,
e_{j}^{\top}(W_{n}(t_{2})-W_{n}(t_{1}))
is a mean-zero random variable and can be written as

\displaystyle e_{j}^{\top}(W_{n}(t_{2})-W_{n}(t_{1}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\{e_{j}^{\top}U_{0i}(s,x)+e_{j}^{\top}U_{1i}(s,x)-\mathbb{E}[e_{j}^{\top}U_{0i}(s,x)+e_{j}^{\top}U_{1i}(s,x)]\}ds,

where, with
z_{s}=\mathfrak{R}(s,x)
,

\displaystyle U_{0i}(s,x)

\displaystyle=(\Phi(s))^{-1}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\frac{\delta-v(s,z_{s})}{p_{s}(z_{s})}p_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)\frac{K_{z_{s}-s\delta,h}(X_{0i}-(z_{s}-s\delta))}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z_{s}-s\delta,h})}d\delta

\displaystyle U_{1i}(s,x)

\displaystyle=(\Phi(s))^{-1}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\frac{\delta-v(s,z_{s})}{p_{s}(z_{s})}p_{0}(z_{s}-s\delta)\frac{K_{z_{s}+(1-s)\delta,h}(X_{1i}-(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta))}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z_{s}-s\delta,h})}d\delta.

Given the independence of
(X_{01},\ldots,X_{0n})
and
(X_{11},\ldots,X_{1n})
, and the similar structure of
U_{0i},U_{1i}
, it suffices to control the second moment of
\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e_{j}^{\top}U_{0i}(s,x)ds
. For this, we use

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e_{j}^{\top}U_{0i}(s,x)ds\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\frac{e_{j}^{\top}(\delta-v(s,z_{s}))}{p_{s}(z_{s})}p_{1}(z_{s}+(1-s)\delta)\frac{K_{z_{s}-s\delta,h}(z-(z_{s}-s\delta))}{\mbox{Vol}(V_{z_{s}-s\delta,h})}d\delta ds\right)^{2}p_{0}(z)dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{h^{2d}}\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\mathbf{1}\{\|z-(z_{s}-s\delta)\|\leq h\}d\delta ds\right)^{2}p_{0}(z)dz,

where the last inequality follows several fact (a)
\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq\overline{\mathfrak{p}},
(b)
|e_{j}^{\top}(\delta-v(s,z))|\leq 3\mbox{diam}(\Omega)
, (c) there exists
\varepsilon_{x}>0
such that
\mathfrak{R}(s,x)\in\Omega^{-\varepsilon_{x}}
for almost all
x\in\Omega^{\circ}
, (d)
p_{s}(z)
is bounded away from for
z\in\Omega^{-\varepsilon}
, (e)
|K_{z,h}(u)|\leq\mathfrak{K}\mathbf{1}\{u\in V_{z,h}\}
, and (f)
\mbox{Vol}(V_{z,h})\geq Ch^{d}
for all
z\in\Omega
.
Note that

\displaystyle\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\mathbf{1}\{\|z-(z_{s}-s\delta)\|\leq h\}d\delta

\displaystyle=\mbox{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}\left(\frac{z_{s}-z}{s},\,\frac{h}{s}\right)\cap\left(\frac{z_{s}-\Omega}{s}\right)\cap\left(\frac{\Omega-z_{s}}{1-s}\right)\right).

Because
s\geq t_{1}\geq h
, we have

\mbox{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}\left(\frac{z_{s}-z}{s},\,\frac{h}{s}\right)\cap\left(\frac{z_{s}-\Omega}{s}\right)\cap\left(\frac{\Omega-z_{s}}{1-s}\right)\right)\leq\mbox{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}\left(\frac{z-z_{s}}{s},\,\frac{h}{s}\right)\right)\leq C(h/s)^{d}.

(E.103)

This implies that

\sup_{z\in\Omega}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\mathbf{1}\{\|z-(z_{s}-s\delta)\|\leq h\}d\delta ds\leq Ch^{d}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\frac{ds}{s^{d}}\leq C(t_{2}-t_{1})(h/t_{1})^{d}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e_{j}^{\top}U_{0i}(s,x)ds\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{h^{2d}}\frac{h^{d}(t_{2}-t_{1})}{t_{1}^{d}}\int_{\Omega}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\mathbf{1}\{\|z-(z_{s}-s\delta)\|\leq h\}p_{0}(z)d\delta dsdz

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{\prime}(t_{2}-t_{1})}{h^{d}t_{1}^{d}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\mathbb{P}(X_{0}\in\mathcal{B}(z_{s}-s\delta,h))d\delta ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(t_{2}-t_{1})}{t_{1}^{d}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}ds=C^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\frac{(t_{2}-t_{1})^{2}}{t_{1}^{d}}.

Hence, we conclude that for
d\geq 2

\begin{split}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\left(\sum_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[|e_{j}^{\top}(W_{n}(t_{2})-W_{n}(t_{1})|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}&\leq C\left(\sum_{t_{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}\sum_{t_{2}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}:|t_{2}-t_{1}|\leq 3/2^{k}}\frac{(t_{2}-t_{1})^{2}}{t_{1}^{d}}\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}}\left(\sum_{t_{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}\frac{1}{t_{1}^{d}}\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}}\left(\sum_{i=\lceil 2^{k}t^{*}\rceil}^{2^{k}}\left(\frac{2^{k}}{i}\right)^{d}\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}}\left(2^{kd}\int_{2^{k}t^{*}}^{2^{k}}\frac{1}{s^{d}}ds\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{2^{k}}\left(2^{kd}\frac{(2^{k}t^{*})^{-d+1}}{d-1}\right)^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{2^{k/2}(t^{*})^{(d-1)/2}}.\end{split}

(E.104)

Substituting this in (
E.102
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\|W_{n}(t)\|\right]\leq C_{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n(t^{*})^{d-1}}}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{2^{k/2}}{2^{k}}\leq\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{n(t^{*})^{d-1}}}.

If
d=1
, then

\sum_{i=\lceil 2^{k}t^{*}\rceil}^{2^{k}}(2^{k}/i)^{d}\leq 2^{k}\int_{2^{k}t^{*}}^{2^{k}}ds/s=2^{k}\log(1/t^{*}).

Hence, we conclude

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[t^{*},1]}\|W_{n}(t)\|\right]\leq C_{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n(t^{*})^{d-1}}}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{2^{k/2}}{2^{k}}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\times\begin{cases}\sqrt{\log(1/t^{*})},&\mbox{if }d=1,\\
(t^{*})^{-(d-1)/2},&\mbox{if }d\geq 2.\end{cases}

The derivation of the bound for
\sup_{t\in[0,t^{*}]}\|Z_{n}(t)\|
follows the same structure and reuse
\mathcal{G}_{k},\mathcal{U}_{k}
for similar sets. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\{\lfloor 2^{k}t\rfloor/2^{k}:\,t\in[0,t^{*})\},\quad\mathcal{U}_{k}:=\{(s,t)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\times\mathcal{G}_{K}:\,|s-t|\leq 3/2^{k}\}.

Clearly, the cardinality of
\mathcal{G}_{k}
is at most
\lceil 2^{k}t^{*}\rceil
and that of
\mathcal{U}_{k}
is at most
6\lceil 2^{k}t^{*}\rceil
. Note that if
k\leq\log_{2}(1/t^{*})
, then
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\{0\}
. Using the same decomposition as (
E.102
), it suffices to control
\mathbb{E}[(e_{j}^{\top}(Z_{n}(t_{2})-Z_{n}(t_{1})))^{2}]
for any
(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
. As before, we get

\mathbb{E}[(e_{j}^{\top}(Z_{n}(t_{2})-Z_{n}(t_{1})))^{2}]\leq\frac{C}{nh^{2d}}\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\mathbf{1}\{\|z-(z_{s}-s\delta)\|\leq h\}d\delta ds\right)^{2}p_{0}(z)dz

However, instead of (
E.103
), we use

\mbox{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}\left(\frac{z_{s}-z}{s},\,\frac{h}{s}\right)\cap\left(\frac{z_{s}-\Omega}{s}\right)\cap\left(\frac{\Omega-z_{s}}{1-s}\right)\right)\leq\mbox{Vol}\left(\left(\frac{z_{s}-\Omega}{s}\right)\cap\left(\frac{\Omega-z_{s}}{1-s}\right)\right)\leq C,

for some constant independent of
s
and depending only on
d
and the diameter of
\Omega
. This implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(e_{j}^{\top}(Z_{n}(t_{2})-Z_{n}(t_{1})))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(t_{2}-t_{1})}{nh^{2d}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{S_{s}(z_{s})}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{1}\{\|z-(z_{s}-s\delta)\|\leq h\}p_{0}(z)dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(t_{2}-t_{1})^{2}}{nh^{d}}.

Note that in decomposition (
E.102
), the summands for
k\leq\log_{2}(1/t^{*})
can be ignored because
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\{0\}
implies
\pi_{k}(t)=\pi_{k-1}(t)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,t^{*}]}\|Z_{n}(t)\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k>\log_{2}(1/t^{*})}\,(\lceil 2^{k}t^{*}\rceil)^{1/2}\max_{1\leq j\leq d}\sup_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|e_{j}^{\top}(Z_{n}(t_{2})-Z_{n}(t_{1})|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k\geq\log_{2}(1/t^{*})}\frac{2^{k/2}(t^{*})^{1/2}}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}}\right)\leq C\frac{t^{*}}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}.

Hence,

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\widetilde{E}^{*}(t,x)\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{t^{*}}{\sqrt{nh^{d}}}\right)+n^{-1/2}\begin{cases}\sqrt{\log(1/t^{*})},&\mbox{if }d=1,\\
(t^{*})^{-(d-1)/2},&\mbox{if }d\geq 2.\end{cases}

For
d=1
, choosing
t^{*}=\sqrt{h\log(1/h)}
and for
d=1
, choosing
t^{*}=h^{d/(d+1)}
, we get

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\widetilde{E}^{*}(t,x)\|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})\times\begin{cases}\sqrt{\log(1/h)},&\mbox{if }d=1,\\
\sqrt{h^{-d(d-1)/(d+1)}},&\mbox{if }d\geq 2.\end{cases}

S.6.12
Proof of Proposition
6

The continuity of
z\mapsto v(0,z)
and
z\mapsto v(1,z)
is obvious, because they are linear functions.

Fix
t\in(0,1/2]
and
z\in\Omega^{\circ}
.

(The case of
t\in[1/2,1)
is similar.) There exists
\varepsilon>0
,

p_{t}(z^{\prime})=\int_{S_{t}(z^{\prime})}p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)p_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)d\delta>0\quad\mbox{for all}\quad z^{\prime}\in B(z,\varepsilon)\subseteq\Omega.

(E.105)

This follows from the assumption that the densities are bounded away from zero on
\Omega
. This calculation allows us to assume that
z,z^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\circ}
and condition (
E.68
) holds.
Moreover, for any
t\in[0,1],

S_{t}(z)
is a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Hence, it suffices to prove that for a continuous function
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
(bounded on bounded sets),

h(z^{\prime})=\int_{S_{t}(z^{\prime})}g(\delta)p_{0}(z^{\prime}-t\delta)p_{1}(z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta)d\delta~\to~h(z)\quad\mbox{as}\quad\|z^{\prime}-z\|\to 0.

(E.106)

For notational convenience, set
e(t,z,\delta)=g(\delta)p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta),
which is non-negative and positive if and only if
\delta\in S_{t}(z)
.
Note that

\displaystyle h(z^{\prime})-h(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{t}(z^{\prime})\setminus S_{t}(z)}e(t,z^{\prime},\delta)d\delta-\int_{S_{t}(z)\setminus S_{t}(z^{\prime})}e(t,z,\delta)d\delta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{S_{t}(z^{\prime})\cap S_{t}(z)}\{e(t,z^{\prime},\delta)-e(t,z,\delta)\}d\delta.

Because
S_{t}(z)
is bounded for all
z
and
g
is bounded on bounded sets, we get

\left|\int_{S_{t}(z^{\prime})\setminus S_{t}(z)}e(t,z^{\prime},\delta)d\delta\right|\leq\|g\|_{\infty}\|p_{0}\|_{\infty}\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}\mbox{Leb}(S_{t}(z^{\prime})\setminus S_{t}(z)).

From Proposition
10
(4), we get for
\|z-z^{\prime}\|=\gamma>0
small enough,

\mbox{Leb}(S_{t}(z^{\prime})\setminus S_{t}(z))\leq\mbox{Leb}((S_{t}(z))^{2\gamma/(t(1-t))}\setminus S_{t}(z))\to 0\quad\mbox{as}\quad\gamma\to 0.

Similarly, we obtain that

\left|\int_{S_{t}(z)\setminus S_{t}(z^{\prime})}e(t,z,\delta)d\delta\right|\to 0\quad\mbox{as}\quad\gamma\to 0.

Finally, setting

\omega_{j}(\varepsilon)=\sup_{\|x-x^{\prime}\|\leq\varepsilon}|p_{j}(x)-p_{j}(x^{\prime})|\quad\mbox{for}\quad j=0,1,

note that

\left|\int_{S_{t}(z^{\prime})\cap S_{t}(z)}\{e(t,z^{\prime},\delta)-e(t,z,\delta)\}d\delta\right|\leq\|g\|_{\infty}\|p_{1}\|_{\infty}\omega_{0}(\gamma)+\|g\|_{\infty}\|p_{0}\|_{\infty}\omega_{1}(\gamma),

which converges to zero as
\gamma\to 0.
(Recall that assumption
(B2)
implies continuity of
p_{0},p_{1}
on the compact
\Omega
, and hence they are uniformly continuous on
\Omega
which yields
\omega_{j}(\gamma)\to 0
as
\gamma\to 0
.)
Therefore, (
E.106
) holds, which by (
E.105
) implies continuity of
z\mapsto v(t,z)
on
\Omega^{\circ}.

Fix
t\in(0,1)
and
z\in\mathrm{SC}(\Omega)
.

Proposition
10
(5) implies that
S_{t}(z)=\{0\}
. Hence,
p_{t}(z)=0
because
p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)=0
almost everywhere
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. To show that
v(t,z)=0
is the continuous extension, we show that for any
z^{\prime}\in\Omega
that is “close” to
z
,
S_{t}(z^{\prime})
is a small set (in the sense of its diameter).

For any
z^{\prime}\in\Omega
, we shall show that

\sup_{\delta^{\prime}\in S_{t}(z^{\prime})}\|\delta^{\prime}\|~\leq~\frac{\|z^{\prime}-z\|+\mathfrak{m}_{z}^{-1}(\|z-z^{\prime}\|/2;\,\Omega)}{\min\{t,1-t\}}.

(E.107)

If
\|\delta^{\prime}\|\leq\|z^{\prime}-z\|/\min\{t,1-t\}
, then nothing to prove. Assume that
\|\delta^{\prime}\|\geq\|z^{\prime}-z\|/\min\{t,1-t\}
.
Applying the definition of modulus of uniform convexity (
75
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mbox{dist}\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}-t\delta^{\prime}}{2},\,\partial\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathfrak{m}_{z}\left(\|z^{\prime}-t\delta^{\prime}-z\|;\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\mbox{dist}\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta^{\prime}}{2},\,\partial\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathfrak{m}_{z}\left(\|z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta^{\prime}-z\|;\Omega\right).

From Lemma
12
, these inequalities imply

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{2}-z\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\mbox{dist}\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{2},\,\partial\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq(1-t)\mbox{dist}\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}-t\delta^{\prime}}{2},\,\partial\Omega\right)+t\mbox{dist}\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta^{\prime}}{2},\,\partial\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq(1-t)\mathfrak{m}_{z}(\|z^{\prime}-t\delta^{\prime}-z\|;\,\Omega)+t\mathfrak{m}_{z}(\|z^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta^{\prime}-z\|;\,\Omega)

\displaystyle\geq\mathfrak{m}_{z}(\min\{t,1-t\}\|\delta^{\prime}\|-\|z^{\prime}-z\|;\,\Omega),

where the last inequality follows from the monotonicity of
\varepsilon\mapsto\mathfrak{m}_{z}(\varepsilon,\,\Omega)
. Therefore,

\min\{t,1-t\}\|\delta^{\prime}\|-\|z^{\prime}-z\|\leq\mathfrak{m}_{z}^{-1}\left(\frac{\|z-z^{\prime}\|}{2};\,\Omega\right).

This implies (
E.107
), which in turn implies that the diameter of
S_{t}(z^{\prime})
converges to zero. Hence, for any
z^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\circ}
with
\|z^{\prime}-z\|\leq\gamma
, we get

\|v(t,z^{\prime})\|\leq\frac{2\max\{\gamma,\mathfrak{m}_{z}^{-1}(\gamma/2;\Omega)\}}{t(1-t)}\to 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad\gamma\to 0.

(Recall that
v(t,z^{\prime})
is the expected value of a random vector supported on
S_{t}(z^{\prime})
.)
Hence, defining
v(t,z)=0
for
t\in(0,1)
and
z\in\partial\Omega
is a point of strict convexity of
\Omega
. Even if we consider
z^{\prime}\in\partial\Omega
with
\|z^{\prime}-z\|\to 0
, Lemma
15
implies that
z^{\prime}
eventually is a point of strict convexity and so, zero is a continuous extension of the velocity field.

S.7
Boundary Effects

The boundary of
\Omega
has a strong effect on
v_{t}(z)
.
When
\Omega
has boundaries,
the velocity
v_{t}(z)
is not differentiable
no matter how smooth the densities are.
Furthermore, the Lipschitz constant that explodes
at
t\to 0
and
t\to 1
.
We note that boundaries
are known to play an important role
for optimal transport maps as well
(Caffarelli 1992, 1996; De Philipps and Figalli 2014)
so it is not surprising that they do so here as well.

Let
\beta
be a positive integer.
We will say that a function
g
is
\beta
-smooth at
x
if,
for all
y
in a neighborhood of
x
, and some
L<\infty
,

|D^{s}g(x)-D^{s}g(y)|\leq L||x-y||\ \ \ \mathrm{for\ all\ }|s|\leq\beta-1.

Thus, for
u
close to
x
,

|g(u)-g_{x,\beta}(u)|\leq L||u-x||^{\beta}

where

g_{x,\beta}(u)=\sum_{|s|<\beta}\frac{(u-x)^{s}}{s!}.

We will use the following facts.

Fact 1
: If

f(z,\delta)
and
g(z,\delta)
are
\beta
-smooth at
(z,\delta)
,

a(z)
and
b(z)
are linear
and

\int_{a_{1}(z)}^{b_{1}(z)}\cdots\int_{a_{d}(z)}^{b_{d}(z)}g(z,\delta)d\delta_{1}\ \cdots d\delta_{d}>0
,
then

\frac{\int_{a_{1}(z)}^{b_{1}(z)}\cdots\int_{a_{d}(z)}^{b_{d}(z)}f(z,\delta)d\delta_{1}\ \cdots d\delta_{d}}{\int_{a_{1}(z)}^{b_{1}(z)}\cdots\int_{a_{d}(z)}^{b_{d}(z)}g(z,\delta)d\delta_{1}\ \cdots d\delta_{d}}

is
\beta
-smooth at
z
.
This follows since, repeated application of the Leibniz rule shows that
the derivates of the numerator and denominator
are linear combinations of

D^{s}f(z,\delta)
,

D^{s}g(z,\delta)
,

D^{s}a_{j}(z)
and
D^{s}b_{j}(z)
.

Fact 2
:
Suppose that
a(z)
is linear for
u<z

and linear for
u>z
and that
the left and right derivatives of
a(u)
at
z
are not equal.
Then

m(z)=\int_{a(z)}^{b(z)}f(z,\delta)d\delta/\int_{a(z)}^{b(z)}g(z,\delta)d\delta
is
not differentiable at
z
.
Similarly for
b(z)
.
This follows by
comparing

m(z+\epsilon)-m(z)
and

m(z)-m(z-\epsilon)
.

We assume throught this section that

p_{0}
and
p_{1}
are
\beta
-smooth at all
z
.
We will consider two examples.
In each case we show the following:

(P1) For

t\in(0,1/2)\bigcap(1/2,1)
,

v_{t}(z)
is
not differentiable on
a set
\Lambda_{t}
of measure 0.
Specifically,

\Lambda_{t}=\Bigl\{z\in\Omega:\ z=(1-t)b_{1}+tb_{2},\ \ \mathrm{for\ some\ }b_{1},b_{2}\in\partial\Omega\Bigr\}.

(E.108)

(P2)
v_{t}(z)
is
\beta
smooth on

\Lambda_{t}^{c}
.

(P3) There is a positive constant
c
such that

C_{t}\equiv\sup_{z_{1},z_{2}}\frac{|v_{t}(z_{2})-v_{t}(z_{1})|}{|z_{2}-z_{1}|}\geq\frac{c}{t(1-t)}.

Case 1: Interval.

\Omega=[\ell,r]
.
Then

v_{t}(z)=\frac{\int_{a_{t}(z)}^{b_{t}(z)}\delta p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}{\int_{a_{t}(z)}^{b_{t}(z)}p_{0}(z-t\delta)p_{1}(z+(1-t)\delta)d\delta}

where

a_{t}(z)=\max\Biggl\{\frac{z-r}{t},\frac{\ell-z}{1-t}\Biggr\},\ \ \ b_{t}(z)=\min\Biggl\{\frac{z-\ell}{t},\frac{r-z}{1-t}\Biggr\}.

If
t<1/2
let

z_{0}=tr+(1-t)\ell
and
z_{1}=t\ell+(1-t)r
.
If
t>1/2
let

z_{1}=tr+(1-t)\ell
and
z_{0}=t\ell+(1-t)r
.
We’ll focus on the case where
t<1/2
.
Then

S_{t}(z)=[a_{t}(z),b_{t}(z)]=\begin{cases}\left[\frac{\ell-z}{1-t},\ \ \frac{z-\ell}{t}\right]&\ell\leq z\leq z_{0}\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\left[\frac{\ell-z}{1-t},\ \ \frac{r-z}{1-t}\right]&z_{0}\leq z\leq z_{1}\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\left[\frac{z-r}{t},\ \ \frac{r-z}{1-t}\right]&z_{1}\leq z\leq r.\end{cases}

Now
a_{t}(z)
and
b_{t}(z)
are linear
on the open intervals

(\ell,z_{0})
,
(z_{0},z_{1})
and
(z_{1},r)

so
v_{t}(z)
is
\beta
-smooth there.
On the other hand,
a_{t}(z)
is not differentiable at
z_{1}
and

b_{z}(t)
is not differentiable at
z_{0}
and so

v_{t}(z)
is not differentiable on
\{z_{0},z_{1}\}
.
So
v_{t}(z)
is not differentiable on

\Lambda_{t}(z)=\{z_{0},z_{1}\}
and is
\beta
-smooth otherwise.
Now

C_{t}\geq\lim_{z\to\ell}\frac{v_{t}(z_{0})-v_{t}(z)}{z_{0}-z}.

For
z<z_{0}
,
and some
\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime}\in(0,z_{0})
,

\displaystyle v_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\frac{\ell-z}{1-t}}^{\frac{z-\ell}{t}}\delta r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}{\int_{\frac{\ell-z}{1-t}}^{\frac{z-\ell}{t}}r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}=\frac{r_{t}(z,\delta^{\prime})\int_{\frac{\ell-z}{1-t}}^{\frac{z-\ell}{t}}\delta d\delta}{r_{t}(z,\delta^{\prime\prime})\int_{\frac{\ell-z}{1-t}}^{\frac{z-\ell}{t}}d\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{t}(z,\delta^{\prime})}{r_{t}(z,\delta^{\prime\prime})}\frac{(z-\ell)(1-2t)}{2t(1-t)}\to 0

as
z\to\ell
.
On the other hand,

v_{t}(z_{0})=\frac{\int_{-t(r-\ell)}^{r-\ell}\delta r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}{\int_{-t(r-\ell)}^{r-\ell}r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}=c+O(t)

where

c=\frac{\int_{0}^{r-\ell}\delta r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}{\int_{0}^{r-\ell}r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}>0.

So

\lim_{z\to\ell}\frac{v_{t}(z_{0})-v_{t}(z)}{z_{0}-\ell}=\frac{c+O(t)}{t(r-z)}\asymp\frac{1}{t}.

Similar arguments hold for the
z\to r
and for
t>1/2
.

Case 2. Rectangle.

\Omega=[\ell_{1},r_{1}]\times\cdots\times[\ell_{d},r_{d}]
.
Without loss of generality, assume that
0<t<1/2
.
Now

S_{t}(z)=[a_{t1}(z),b_{t1}(z)]\times\cdots\times[a_{td}(z),b_{td}(z)]

where

a_{tj}(z)=\max\Biggl\{\frac{z_{j}-r_{j}}{t},\frac{\ell_{j}-z}{1-t}\Biggr\},\ \ \ b_{tj}(z)=\min\Biggl\{\frac{z-\ell_{j}}{t},\frac{r_{j}-z}{1-t}\Biggr\}.

Let

z_{0j}=tr_{j}+(1-t)\ell_{j}
and
z_{1j}=t\ell_{j}+(1-t)r_{j}
.
Then

\ell_{j}\leq z_{0j}\leq z_{1j}\leq r_{j}
.
The interval
[\ell_{j},r_{j}]

is partitioned into three intervals

[\ell_{j},z_{0j}]
,
[z_{0j},z_{1j}]
,
[z_{1j},r_{j}]
.
Then

[a_{tj}(z),b_{tj}(z)]=\begin{cases}\left[\frac{\ell_{j}-z_{j}}{1-t},\ \ \frac{z_{j}-\ell_{j}}{t}\right]&\ell_{j}\leq z_{j}\leq z_{0j}\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\left[\frac{\ell_{j}-z_{j}}{1-t},\ \ \frac{r_{j}-z_{j}}{1-t}\right]&z_{0j}\leq z_{j}\leq z_{1j}\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\left[\frac{z_{j}-r_{j}}{t},\ \ \frac{r_{j}-z_{j}}{1-t}\right]&z_{1j}\leq z_{j}\leq r_{j}.\end{cases}

Hence we can partition
S_{t}(z)

into sets of the form

C_{1}\times\cdots\times C_{d}

where each
C_{j}
is one of

[\ell_{j},z_{0j}]
,
[z_{0j},z_{1j}]
,
[z_{1j},r_{j}]
.
There are
N=3^{d}
such sets
which we denote by
R_{1},\ldots,R_{N}
.
Then

a_{jt}(z)
and
b_{jt}(z)
are linear functions
over the interiors of these sets
but have a change of slope at the boundaries.
So
v_{t}(z)
is smooth
over the interiors of
R_{1},\ldots,R_{N}
.
See Figure
A.1
.

Let
\Lambda_{t}(z)
be the boundaries of these sets.
Note that this is precisely the set defined by (
E.108
).
Fix a point
z
on the boundary of
R_{k}
, say.
There exists at least one
j
such that

z_{j}=z_{0j}
or

z_{j}=z_{1j}
.
Rewrite
S_{t}(z)
as

S_{t}(z)=[a_{tj}(z),b_{tj}(z)]\times B

where

B=\prod_{k\neq j}[a_{tk}(z),b_{tk}(z)]
.
Hence,

\int_{S_{t}(z)}\delta r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta=\int_{a_{tj}(z)}^{b_{tj}(z)}M_{t}(z_{j},\delta)

where

M_{t}(z_{j},\delta)=\int_{B}\delta r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta_{(-j)}

where
\delta_{(-j)}=(\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{j-1},\delta_{j+1},\ldots,\delta_{d})
.
By Fact 2,

\int_{a_{tj}(z)}^{b_{tj}(z)}M_{t}(z_{j},\delta)
is not differentiable.
A similar argument applies to the denominator

\int_{S_{t}(z)}r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta
.

Figure A.1
:
This plot shows the sets
R_{1},\ldots,R_{N}
in the case of a hyper-rectangle.
The set of non-differentiability
\Lambda_{t}
consists of the vertical and horizontal lines.
The function
v_{t}(z)
is
\beta
-smooth in the interior of each
R_{j}
.

Now we bound Lipschitz constant.
Let
z_{n}
be a sequence in
\Omega
such that

z_{n}\to\ell=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{d})
as
n\to\infty
.
It is easy to check that

v_{t}(z_{n})\to 0
as
z_{n}\to\ell

as in the univariate case.
Next,

\displaystyle v_{t}(z_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{-t(r_{1}-\ell_{1})}^{r_{1}-\ell_{1}}\times\int_{-t(r_{d}-\ell_{d})}^{r_{d}-\ell_{d}}\delta r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}{\int_{-t(r_{1}-\ell_{1})}^{r_{1}-\ell_{1}}\times\int_{-t(r_{d}-\ell_{d})}^{r_{d}-\ell_{d}}r_{t}(z,\delta)d\delta}

\displaystyle\to\frac{\int_{0}^{r_{1}-\ell_{1}}\times\int_{0}^{r_{d}-\ell_{d}}\delta p_{1}(z+\delta)d\delta}{\int_{0}^{r_{1}-\ell_{1}}\times\int_{0}^{r_{d}-\ell_{d}}p_{1}(z+\delta)d\delta}\equiv c.

Also,

||z_{0}-\ell||=t||r-\ell||
.
Hence,

C_{t}\geq\frac{v(z_{0})-v(\ell)}{||z_{0}-\ell||}\asymp\frac{c}{t||r-\ell||}

as
t\to 0
.
